- **Fixed on the way, found by the whole-plan review and proven by reading pixels:** the editor's video (and its Bake, and the workflow Frame output) dropped the Frame's background, groups, wired treatments and post effects — a Frame with a red background recorded a black ground; it now keeps them (red corners 254,1,0). The card drew every video frame at time 0, so living images and animated shader fills never moved — now each frame is painted at its moment (frames differ across the loop), and a clip whose frames can't load fails loudly. No background → WebM with real transparency (alpha 0–255). Closing the editor mid-export no longer leaves its live loop running; Bake and "Generate as image" wait during an export.
- **Behaviour change to know:** Bake from the Motion dock now bakes the background and post effects into the frames a workflow run plays (previously transparent). The browser route no longer re-bakes the node's own frame sequence (the fallback still does); the Motion dock's "stale" flag shows when it needs one.
- **Known / parked:** card videos don't include motion bands or letter behaviours (the card has never driven them; a motion-only Frame downloads as a still); odd sizes leave a 1-px edge (the recorder rounds up to even); Cancel reads "Export cancelled." in the editor and "Cancelled" on the card; the server route's upload can't be stopped mid-batch; `recordEmbed` takes the loop length from its caller, not the embed's own.

### Studio videos are recorded in the browser — Shader, Gradient, Space Type, 3D — LANDED 2026-09-22 (`b0c4f1bb8`..`0246f1db8`, subagent-driven, a review per task + a whole-feature review and fix wave)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-21-browser-video-export-design.md`; plan 1 of 3 `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-22-browser-video-export-studios.md`. Plan 2 (Frame/Compositor + the embed bridge) landed the same day — see the entry above; plan 3 (Timeline, with sound) landed too.

- **What changed:** "Download video" and "As video" in the four studios record the video in the browser with `mediabunny` (`app/lib/engine/videoRecorder.ts`) and upload ONE file (`publishVideo.ts`) instead of every frame as a PNG followed by a server encode. Works in hosted mode (it needs only the existing upload). A **Cancel** button appears while a video is being made; it works in every phase, and closing a studio stops its export.
- **Measured, real exports both ways** (dev lab pages, Playwright downloads): Shader 1.7 s vs server 6.6 s; Gradient 2.0 vs 5.1 s; Space Type 1080p60 4.1 vs 24.3 s; 3D 1.6 vs 3.9 s; Space Type transparent 7.9 s vs ~43 s end to end (3.0 MB vs 2.0 MB). Same frame counts, BT.709 on every file, 0.08–1.68 levels apart.
- **If the browser can't record:** local mode falls back to the old server route and SAYS so in the footer ("Made on the server, because …"); hosted mode shows a plain error. **Escape hatch for comparing or if the browser route misbehaves:** `localStorage.setItem('Sailor.VideoExport', 'server')` (local mode only; remove the key to go back).
- **Quality gate kept in the repo:** `tests/browser-video-export.spec.ts` records a known pattern and measures the file against the server's own encode of the same frames (frame count, length, BT.709 limited range, closeness); `tests/studio-video-export.spec.ts` guards the studio wiring (notice, Cancel, download). `tests/tools/compare_videos.py A B` compares any two videos (libvpx for WebM alpha).
- **Found by measuring:** mediabunny's alpha path drops a canvas frame's colour space, so transparent WebM came out BT.601 — the recorder now converts RGBA→I420A with the BT.709 matrix itself (alpha-weighted edge colour, one reused buffer, 4.8 ms/frame at 1080p) and gives VP9 a bitrate (Chrome's quantizer mode spent ~6× the server's bits). The footer now puts buttons before the status text: a changing "Rendering n/120" was sliding Cancel ~10 px every frame.
- **Known / deferred:** a hosted recording over 100 MB is refused only after recording (no size estimate up front); the gate tests alpha at 0 and 255 only; a fallback notice hides "Saved ✓" until the next export; 3D shows export failures in red, the others in grey; Safari/Firefox colour behaviour unproven; the old `/sailor/spacetype_encode` route is retired two weeks after plan 3 if no fallback notice is reported.

### Timeline — exports carry every audio clip; editing behaves like an editor — LANDED 2026-09-21 (`48f34d25c`..`8f8c3d45c`, 10 commits, subagent-driven, a review per task)

Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-20-timeline-editing-upgrade.md` (its "Build notes" record where the build departed from it). Started as a survey of open-source editors (opencut-classic, WebAV, Omniclip, mediabunny); ideas were borrowed and re-derived with tests, no code copied.

- **Export sound was broken and is fixed.** The server only ever added the FIRST audio clip, from 0 s, at full volume, no fades. The browser now mixes every audio clip (all tracks, position, volume, fades, speed, reverse) into one WAV with the same maths the preview plays, uploads it, and the unchanged exporter adds it. Measured on a real export: the second track was absent before (0.000) and present after (0.150); a 2-track mix rendered tone levels 0.300 / 0.075 / 0.150 exactly as predicted. One file per timeline (`timeline_mix_<node id>.wav`), overwritten each export.
- **Preview audio honours speed and reverse** (it ignored both). Sped-up audio changes pitch, like a tape, in preview and export alike.
- **Group snap**: a moving selection snaps on any edge of any member and never on itself. **Group trim**: trimming one selected clip trims them all by one shared amount, limited once by the tightest clip. **A trim stops at the neighbouring clip.**
- **No accidental overlaps**: a clip dropped on another hops to the nearest free track of the same kind, or gets a new one — only when it really moved, so clicking a clip in an older, already-overlapping timeline leaves it alone.
- **Ripple switch** (toolbar, off by default, remembered): when on, trimming or deleting slides the later clips on that track so no gap or overlap is left; applied on mouse release, one undo step. Also fixed: deleting a multi-selection left transitions pointing at deleted clips.
- Two traps found and written into the code comments: Web Audio's DynamicsCompressor adds ~7% makeup gain to everything (so no limiter node — `fitPeak` turns a mix down only when it would clip); Sailor's `/view` route keeps a permanent copy of every file it serves, so "does this uploaded file still exist" cannot be asked through it.
- New pure modules: `app/lib/engine/audio/mixdown.ts`, `shared/timeline/{groupEdit,placement,ripple,edits}.ts` (`edits` holds whole edits the editor runs from several places — delete, restoring a transition after a drag — so keyboard, toolbar and right-click menu all do the same thing and the tests run the real code). 163 unit tests across the 14 timeline-related specs.
- **OWED:** the drag behaviours (group snap, group trim, track hop, ripple on release) have not been tried with a real mouse in the running app — the browser pane was not on screen during the build.
- **Follow-ups from the whole-feature review — DONE 2026-09-21:** adding, pasting or duplicating a clip onto occupied frames now hops it to a free track (one undo step), and so does slowing a clip into its neighbour (with ripple on, the later clips follow instead); Delete follows the Ripple switch and Cmd+Delete always closes the gap, both for a whole selection and through one code path; an audio clip whose file is missing is left out of the mix and counted in a notice, instead of sending the whole export back to first-clip-only sound; the mix filename is unique per browser tab as well as per timeline; the export button says "Mixing sound…"; a transition follows its two clips when they hop together, and a clip moved to another track by hand no longer keeps a transition pointing at the old one. The right-click menu's Delete now goes through the same path as the keyboard (it used to delete clips on locked tracks and ignore the Ripple switch); a locked track no longer accepts a dropped asset. **Still open:** sending a clip to a timeline whose editor is not open writes the clip straight into the saved state and skips the no-overlap hop.
- **Done 09-22 (see "Timeline export records in the browser" above):** export in the browser (mediabunny). Timeline export was switched off entirely in hosted mode; the feasibility read found the WebGL preview already has an awaitable, frame-exact `renderFrame` held pixel-close to the Python exporter by a golden test, and that today's export silently drops title and lower-third clips, which the preview draws.

### Kinetic Studio — Showcase: 35 card layouts, each its own effect; two-tab gallery — LANDED 2026-09-20 (`8c0ec71d4`..`d3d9ac7f1`, 5 commits)

**What:** the animos.app adaptation started in August, finished. Showcase (photos, fill cards and words riding one arrangement) went from 4 layouts under one `ring` effect to **35 layouts, each a first-class effect** with its own gallery entry, dials, saved default and embed bundle. The effect gallery splits into **Layouts** (35, sectioned by family, thumbnails that play on hover) and **Text** (the type-first effects). A fresh Showcase opens on a dozen fill cards, not words.

**How it holds.** One host, a factory: `effects/showcase.ts` → `makeShowcaseEffect({ id, layout })`. `ring` keeps its id (saved scenes); the other 34 are `show<layout>` because `tunnel`, `spiral`, `cascade` and `turntable` are also text effects. One five-line module per entry, because the embed build reads one effect id per module file — and **every factory call carries `/* @__PURE__ */`**: a bare top-level call is a side effect Rollup must keep, which put the whole host and all 35 layouts into *every* per-effect bundle (Ball's included) with the embed suite green. Two guards now: a source check on every entry module, and a `vortexRings` marker in the embed split test (`ring` alone may carry every layout — it still draws scenes saved as `ring` + `params.layout`, and the editor re-homes those on open).

**A layout is pure placement** (`layouts/*.ts`: `place(i, n, params, t01)` → position, yaw/pitch/roll, scale, opacity) plus its own pose, bend radius and back-fade depth. **One function, `travel()`, shapes every layout's movement** — so Pacing (layout default / continuous / step per card), ten Easings and Hold (shown only under step per card: never a dead dial), fractional Speed (the engine plays as many loops as the travel needs to close) and Direction "There and back" (closes every loop) reach all 35 without per-layout code. Host-side: a soft shadow quad per card (no texture; hidden from behind; bends with the card), Pulse (timed so a ring peaks on the nearest card), and faded words that fade instead of vanishing.

**The panel reads in decision order:** Layout (only that layout's own dials — shape, counts, spacing, angles) → Content (card list, Repeater) → Cards → Type (only while the content holds text, via a new `showIf.matches`) → Look → Motion tab (shared dials, then the layout's own; animation is only authored there). A unit test asserts no two dials on a panel share a name. Switching between two layouts keeps content, card look and motion (`carryKeys`), outranking the target's saved default scene.

**Thumbnails are not captures:** `layouts/thumbnail.ts` projects each layout's own maths with the engine's camera onto a 2D canvas (unit-tested equal to three.js) — no WebGL contexts, no drift, free for a new layout.

**Also fixed on the way:** a Showcase wired into another studio drew blank photo cards (only the studio surface preloaded images; the card and headless engines now do, per engine); the Space Type panel never passed `optionLabels` to its selects; "Reset to defaults" passed its click event as an argument.

**Verified:** 900+ spacetype unit tests (every layout: finite, seamless, closes at fractional speeds and under six pacing/easing/direction variants); real-browser renders of all 35; both gallery tabs driven with Playwright; all 63 embed bundles rebuilt and the split confirmed by content. **Owed (live, in the studio):** the carry-over on a layout switch, the re-homing of an old scene, the wired-image fix with a photo card feeding a Frame, and the feel of the easing presets. Default sizes and poses are a first pass. Five animos templates (Zoom parallax and the four Reveal & wipe) are full-frame masked transitions and are not expressible as card placement.

### fal is the default provider — every inpaint and vector route moved off Replicate — LANDED 2026-09-11 (`9b0c8fbd9`..`731ffde79`, 6 commits, three implementers in parallel on disjoint files, a review each, one follow-up commit)

**What:** on "i'd rather fal be the default" and then "yes move them all to fal". Every `runReplicate` call under the server's API routes now runs on fal, with the response shapes unchanged so no client moved: background removal → `fal-ai/birefnet/v2`; Kontext edits → `fal-ai/flux-kontext/dev`; the four text-to-image tiers → `fal-ai/flux/schnell`, `fal-ai/flux/dev`, `fal-ai/bytedance/seedream/v4.5/text-to-image`, `fal-ai/flux-2-pro`; pose transfer → `fal-ai/nano-banana-2/edit`; FLUX Fill's dev tier → `fal-ai/flux-lora/inpainting` (the caller's 30-scale guidance is mapped to fal's CFG by ÷8, clamped 1–10); Nano Banana Pro is fal-only (the Replicate primary and the failover wrapper are gone); Recraft vector generate → `fal-ai/recraft/v3/text-to-image` (vector styles return SVG) and vectorize → `fal-ai/recraft/vectorize`. **LoRA inference** → `fal-ai/flux-lora`: each trained LoRA lives on Replicate as a ~340 MB `trained_model.tar`, so the first generation per LoRA streams the tar to disk, lifts `output/flux_train_replicate/lora.safetensors` out with the venv's `tarfile` (`scripts/lora_extract_safetensors.py`), uploads it to fal storage once and caches `fal_weights_url` on the sidecar — later runs are instant; the 65 sidecars migrate lazily, never in a batch. A shared `falImageSize(aspect, longSide)` turns the routes' aspect strings into fal's presets or a 16-aligned box (Seedream needs a 3200 long side to clear its minimum). Every new fal slug has a price row. Payloads are pure builders (`inpaintFalInputs.ts`, `recraftFalInputs.ts`, `loraGenInput.ts`) pinned by unit tests against fal's documented schemas.

**What the reviews caught:** the FLUX Fill dev tier crossed to fal without the empty-prompt guard its pro tier already had — fal 400s on an empty prompt and the Remove button and outpaint-fit send one — fixed inside the builder and pinned; the LoRA tar download had no timeout, so a stalled connection would have pinned that LoRA's in-flight memo forever — bounded to ten minutes and https-only. Noted: `line_circuit` has no confirmed fal sub-style and falls back to plain vector illustration; the LoRA upload's peak memory is roughly three times the file (fal storage copies the buffer); an unknown text-to-image tier silently bills as schnell.

**Still on Replicate, deliberately:** LoRA *training* (cloud-train), voice clone, lip-sync speech, the Krea prompt rewrite (an LLM, which fal does not host), and the two cover-image fetches. Julien's call whether those move.

**Owed:** one cheap paid smoke per route — Cut out subject (~half a cent), a Kontext edit, one schnell render, one vectorize — the classifier blocks paid clicks in the browser pane, so these are Julien's.

### Living image clip — a Frame still becomes a looping, transparent clip — LANDED 2026-09-10 (`ca3652207`..`536207151`, 14 commits, subagent-driven, a review per task, a whole-feature review and its fix wave)

**What:** on "how can we make the roses get animated in a loop?" — an image layer in the Frame keeps its still and may carry a **clip**: a folder of PNG frames with real transparency under `input/sailor_clips/`. The Frame draws the frame for its own clock, so the layer moves in the Design tab, under the Motion playhead, on the canvas card and in the bake; the clip's played length joins the master-clock reconciliation so "Generate as video" picks a whole-cycle length and the loop closes; each cloner copy starts the clip at a different moment (a new **Phase** dial, 1 = spread evenly, 0 = unison — Julien's rose is one element cloned four times, so one clip animates four out-of-step roses). A **Speed** dial (0.25×–4×) rescales playback and the loop length with it. The clip is made by a new route (`POST /api/frame/animate`): flatten the still onto chroma green (blue if the still is more than 3 % green), send it as **first AND last frame** to Seedance or Hailuo on fal (Luma on Replicate uses its native loop flag), then `scripts/clip_key.py` keys every frame back to transparency (chroma distance with a soft ramp, spill suppression on edge pixels) guarded by the still's own silhouette dilated 4 %, and writes the folder. An **Animate** section holds prompt, model (Luma / Seedance / Hailuo), length, the flat price on the Generate button, Speed and Remove clip. **Moved 2026-09-13 (`7df4bd4e6`)** on Julien's "shouldn't the animate card live in the motion tab": it was in the Design tab's layer inspector (under the Displacement map block); it now sits in the **Motion tab** above `MotionLayerEditor` under the selection-aware "Layer motion", because a clip is how the layer moves and composes with its keyframes — a pure template move, same image-only gate, handlers unchanged. **Takes, 2026-09-13 (`5cbab3488`)** on "even if i re-roll the animation, i should be able to go back to a previous generation": a re-roll used to overwrite `layer.clip` and orphan the old folder. Every generation is now appended to `layer.takes` (oldest first, `withTake` in lib/compositor/clip, capped at 12 references — folders are never deleted); the Animate card shows a **Takes** row of frame-0 thumbnails once there is something to go back to (a second take, or a removed clip whose take remains), the active one outlined, click restores it with its own remembered Speed; Remove clip keeps the takes. Layers are stored raw, so `takes` round-trips like `clip` did. 36 unit tests across the helpers and the panel. Frame video export now passes the alpha flag when the background is No Fill, so a transparent WebM comes out. **Byte-identity:** a layer without a clip paints exactly as before — every new branch is gated on `layer.clip`, proven through the real `paintLayerStack` with a recording context (mutation-checked: freezing the clone slot or the clock fails it).

**What the reviews caught:** the silhouette LRU baked a clip's first frame forever on a layer with a torn edge or feather, and the per-`paintLayer` DOF/silhouette memos collapsed the clone offset (a clip now opts out of both); a broken folder was re-fetched in full on every layer edit (a failed set); the cache was keyed by dir alone (now dir + frame count); the three model keys the route dispatches were **unpriced** so hosted mode would have refused every call (rows added; the hold is **flat per model** because `resolveCredits` prefers the price book over any hint — so the button now quotes the flat row too, the final review having caught a 4 s clip quoted at $0.48 and charged $0.60); the image input had no type or size gate (PNG-only, 12 MB, magic bytes); `_clipCache` was unbounded at ~420 MB for a default 5 s 720p clip (swept against the current layers, capped at two, never evicting a clip the frame still shows); the route guessed the input dir as `cwd/..` when the repo already had `engineDirForType`; the Luma path uploaded the still to fal storage for nothing (changed to a data URL — and reverted the same evening: Julien's first press got Replicate's E006 "Start image must start with http:// or https://", so Luma takes the fal public URL like the fal models; no charge, the hold released). The plan's soft-alpha test sample sat outside its own ramp — `(40, 230, 40)` is the one that lands inside. **Browser-verified** on the frame lab with a 24-frame dev clip and a 2×2 cloner: all frames load through `/view?subfolder=…`, the copies pulse out of step, the Motion clock reconciles (4 s with a shader slot), the Animate section renders and Remove clip restores the still — which found a clip whose model id is not in the catalog leaving the select blank (fixed).

**The first paid run (Julien, Seedance 5 s, "the rose should flutter") worked and keyed badly:** the model painted an olive/tan halo (RGB ≈ 207,189,93) right around the subject, inside the guard ring and ~60–90 Lab units from any green, so the chroma test kept it as a rim around every rose. Reworked the same evening (`5c3bb9473`, `fe83a810a`, `c107872d0`): the key colour is now **sampled from the frame's far background** per frame (a median, with the tolerance taken from that ground's own spread) and measured in Lab; in the guard ring a **subject-similarity gate** keeps a pixel only if it resembles the nearest subject colour in the still (a moved petal survives at alpha 1.0, the olive ring drops from 90 % surviving to 0.4 % on a synthetic rose at production size); the matte is eroded one pixel and softened; the guard shrank to 2.5 % and now dilates by iterated shifts (12 s → 0.04 s); the prompt asks for a "solid green screen (chroma key)" ground, a term the models know; and the clip folder keeps `source.mp4`, with a `rekey` CLI so tuning never costs another run. **Luma dropped 2026-09-11 on Julien's "luma is a bit useless here":** the Animate section now offers two rows, labelled with their versions — **Seedance 2.0** (ByteDance, on fal, 4–12 s) and **Hailuo H3** (MiniMax, on fal, 5/6/10 s, 768p); both loop by first = last frame, the Replicate branch and the price row are gone. **Then, on "are there more advanced versions?" (same day):** two rows added from a live read of the fal catalog — **Hailuo H3 Max** (fal's own retrain of H3, ranked first for quality, 768p, 5–15 s, ~$0.40 per 5 s from 15 Sep; the endpoint the repo's video catalog already wires) and **Kling 3.0 Pro** (`fal-ai/kling-video/v3/pro/image-to-video`, `start_image_url` + `end_image_url`, duration as a string enum 3–15, audio off; 1080p per Kling's spec, ~$0.56 per 5 s with audio off) — so the dropdown offers four models, each labelled **name (resolution · flat price)** on Julien's ask. **2026-09-12, on "instead of $ can we use credits" (`5295ecd0f`):** the label and the Generate button quote **credits** — 90 / 45 / 60 / 84 — pinned on each catalog row and tested equal to the server `MODEL_COSTS` row the route is metered against, so the quote is the hold; hand-set rather than run through `creditsForUsd`, because 0.56 × 150 lands a hair above 84 in floating point and the formula would ceil Kling to 85. **2026-09-12, on "[FLUX video] both please" (`dd1e636d9`):** the standalone text-to-video half already existed — FLUX 3 is a full row in the "Generate a video" node, `provider="fal"`, `blackforestlabs/flux-3`+`text-to-video`, priced flat at $2.00 in `VIDEO_MODEL_USD` — so only the Animate half was built: a fifth clip model **FLUX 3 draft** (`flux-3-draft`), calling fal's dedicated `blackforestlabs/flux-3/first-last-frame-to-video/draft` (720p, ~$0.06/s), where the loop the other rows are coaxed into is native. Frames are `start_image_url`/`end_image_url`, duration an INTEGER, resolution a lowercase string (matching `video_models.py`'s FLUX 3 builder); `generate_audio` is omitted — the endpoint has no such field and a keyed loop has no use for it, and an unknown field 422s at fal. Priced flat per 5 s at 45 credits in MODEL_COSTS. A distinct id keeps it off the video node's full-quality `flux-3`. **OWED: one paid smoke** — the exact first-last-frame contract (audio field, int-vs-string duration) is unconfirmed and a fal slug/enum mismatch passes at submit, fails at result ([[fal-enum-mismatch-silent-fallover]]). Seedance 2.5 (July 2026) was looked at and left out: it buys length (30 s) and reference capacity, not resolution, at roughly four times the price. **Second paid run, Julien: "that is a lot better"** — the halo is gone on real frames. **Still to judge by eye:** Frame → select the rose → Animate → Luma, 5 s → Generate (~$0.40), then judge edge fringe (raise `KEY_HI` for green fringe, lower `KEY_LO` for eaten petals), the wrap on Luma (`trim_last` is off for Luma on the assumption its loop flag returns no duplicate last frame), and a transparent export. **Debt:** a clip is capped at the video's resolution (a 2048 px still gets a 1280 px clip and softens the moment it moves); regenerating orphans the old folder on disk; the guard's MaxFilter costs 7 s at 720p (a downscale-dilate-upscale would be milliseconds); the decode materialises every frame (~800 MB for 12 s); a keying failure after a paid download throws the mp4 away; the Phase dial shows on every cloner, clip or not; the "small warning on the layer row" for a missing folder is not built (the still shows instead).

### A shape-following effect leaked its silhouette across surfaces — FIXED 2026-09-11 (`a5250ebb5`)

**What:** Julien applied Liquid metal to a clover in the Compositor, then opened 3D Studio and the metal material on a dodecahedron was **clover-shaped too**. Both surfaces render a catalog shader through one shared renderer (`shaderFx`) via `field.ts`; its per-program uniforms persist across draws, and `buildPasses` never wrote `u_hasShape` on a render with no shape. So the Compositor's glass paint left `u_hasShape=1` and the layer silhouette bound on the program, and the next render of the same effect that passed no shape — a 3D material, a Space Type fill — inherited them. Fixed: `buildPasses` now always emits `u_hasShape:0`, and only the Frame's glass paint overrides it to 1 (with the silhouette). Inert for effects that don't declare the uniform, exactly like `u_hasInput`. Server goldens unaffected (they render off the ComfyUI path, not `field.ts`). Proven in the harness — a shape render followed by a no-shape render of the same effect no longer leaks: the second is a balanced circle (top 10383 / bottom 7025), not the first's top-only silhouette (top 4609 / bottom 0).

### Shader library: a Material category, Liquid metal first — LANDED 2026-09-11 (`40951ac28`)

**What:** Julien's ask, refined by him — a **Material** category in the shader library, not a section in the shape library. Liquid metal's manifest category is `material`, and `SHADER_SECTIONS` gained a Material entry, so the Shader Studio catalog modal and every shader-fill effect picker list it under Material (the fill editor derives the label from the category, so no per-picker wiring). The earlier shape-library Material rail and its `lib/shapes/materials.ts` catalog were removed; `ShapePicker` and `CompositorModal` are back to their pre-material state. **Liquid metal** stays a normal input-sampling, `followsShape` catalog effect, and the general own-fill fix (`isShapeFollowingOwnFill` routes a shape-following effect through the glass paint path even on "Its own fill") stays too, so picking it as a shader fill on a Frame shape fills the real outline either way. A port of the Shaders.com LiquidMetal component (the Fluid Chrome design's values): molten folds (Turbulence, Ripple, Swirl) bend a reflected studio (Environment, rotation, Sharpness, Dispersion) and the picture beneath, a key light glints on them, an edge bevel follows the outline (Bevel width, Bevel profile), Light and Dark colours tint the metal. Verified live in the Shader Studio catalog modal — the Material filter shows one effect, Liquid metal, with a chrome thumbnail. **Look not judged by Julien yet.** The manifest carried a parallel session's uncommitted slice_shift edit throughout; every commit staged only its own hunks via a private index.

**A material fills the whole surface — LANDED 2026-09-11 (`8420b20ca`).** Julien, five-plus times: Liquid metal on a 3D Studio Plane "renders as a circle", he wants it "applied to the entirety of the shape". The deeper issue: it was built with the Center/Radius circle copied from the lens ports, so wherever no silhouette is handed over (3D Studio, Space Type, the catalog preview all render with `u_hasShape=0` after the leak fix), it drew its stand-alone disc. But a *material* coats whatever it is on. Fix is in `liquid_metal.frag` only: the no-shape branch is now full-bleed — every texel deep interior (`cover=1`, `edgeD=1`), molten chrome corner to corner, no border bevel (a UV-border bevel would seam a sphere or a wrapped glyph; the mesh's own edges are the outline). The Frame's shape branch (`u_hasShape=1`) is untouched, so following a Frame layer's silhouette is byte-identical. This is the right split: a **lens** (Glass lens, Crystal) is a disc and keeps its bounded no-shape circle; a **material** fills. Center X/Y and Radius were the disc's controls, now dead — removed from the manifest and, with `centerParam` and their three uniform decls, from the frag (the manifest↔frag parity spec is bidirectional). Verified headless with `render_effect` on a 360×220 canvas: the metal fills the full non-square frame, corners matching the interior, no disc. No `scene3d/materials.ts` change needed.

### Studio backdrop — a lit photographer's cove — LANDED 2026-09-11 (`2278337c4`)

**What:** `studio_backdrop`, the fourth Shaders.com port from Julien's preview link (the StudioBackground component). A generative, animated Shader Studio effect: a wall folding into a floor (Wall curvature), an overhead key beam that widens up the wall and pools on the floor at the Stage centre (the draggable handle; Light depth aims it at the wall or the floor), two side fills with spread and softness, a back wash climbing the wall from the fold, an Ambient level, faint drifting ambient lights on the wrapper's clock (Ambient detail; the pattern seeds from the built-in seed, so no Seed dial), and a Vignette; three light colours plus the surface colour. The percent dials keep the component's 0–100 ranges so a design's numbers carry over as they are. Built as a 2D lighting composition after a 3D cove reconstruction rendered the fold as a bright stripe. Matched to the reference by pixel samples at the corners, the wall, the beam's top and foot, the pool and the floor edge (all within a few levels; the beam's foot was clipping to white and was softened). Proven by server renders, targeted goldens, browser-harness renders that match the server pixel for pixel, a six-second drift diff, and the studio clock gate. **Judged by eye: not yet.** Lights multiply the surface colour, so a dark surface stays dim under the key — the component's own model, but worth a look on a black cyc.

### Crystal — a cut gem of mirrored, ringed facets — LANDED 2026-09-10 (`fd3f80128`)

**What:** `crystal`, the third Shaders.com port from the same preview link (the Crystal component, a ten-sided polygon with eleven-fold facets, refraction 1.14, dispersion 0.99, inner zoom 1.5, light from the top). A lens-category, shape-following effect like Glass lens: the angle around the centre is folded into Facets mirrored sectors, the radius into ten rings tighter near the centre, and each ring is cut into near-equilateral triangular faces (as many per ring as keep a cell about as wide as its ring is tall — the first draft used a fixed count and rendered as thin spokes). Every face has a seeded flat normal turned back into the world by its sector, so Light angle lands on one side of the gem: Highlights add hard specular hits (a narrow specular lobe, so most faces stay dark and a few go white, as the reference does), Shadows darken the faces turned away, Brightness pushes toward white. Each face shifts its slice of the picture along its normal and integrates it across the visible spectrum with the same Zucconi fit `crystal_prism` uses. Sides and Radius describe the stand-alone polygon; in a Frame the layer's silhouette height field takes over. Tint (zero in the design) not ported. Proven by a server render on the design's dark backdrop and the test card, targeted goldens, four browser-harness renders, and an ellipse in the frame lab whose facets run to the ellipse's own edge. **Julien's first look: "it doesn't look like it's taking the shape" — right: in shape mode the rings were still measured as a circle of the half short side, so a rectangle got a round gem with a flat band outside it. Fixed (`2000d9493`): the rings come from the silhouette's depth (0 on the outline, 1 at the middle), and the Frame's hand-over blurs the silhouette across the whole shape for an effect with no Thickness dial (Glass lens keeps its rim band). Verified on a rotated rounded rectangle: rectangular rings, facets to the corners.** Julien's second look, on a star: still a circle — the arms showed plain backdrop. Coverage and the rings both read one blurred silhouette, and a star's thin arms blur below the halfway level. Fixed (`8b656c16e`): the hand-over texture packs two fields, R the rim (blurred by the thickness) and G the true distance to the outline (a chamfer transform on a 128 px thumbnail); coverage reads G in both lens shaders, Crystal's rings read G, Glass lens keeps its rim band from R. Verified on two five-point stars, one per effect. **Still to judge: the hits sit more on the light side than scattered all round, and over bright content the gem saturates toward white (Brightness does that by design); both are constant tunes, not dials.

### Glass lens follows the layer's shape — LANDED 2026-09-10 (`6f814aa6c`)

**What:** on Julien's note that the lens drew a circle inside his rectangle: a lens effect can now declare `followsShape` in the manifest, and the Frame's glass paint (`applyGlassFromLayer`) builds the layer's silhouette *first* and hands it to the shader as a soft height field — the white-on-black silhouette blurred by the glass thickness, so 0.5 sits on the edge and 1.0 one thickness inside — plus the bounds' centre and half short side (read off a 64 px thumbnail, so any shape, rotation or mask works). The shader takes its edge, bend, outward normal and highlight from that field; Center and Radius only matter stand-alone. The renderer gained a **live-texture slot** (one GL texture per uniform name, re-uploaded every render, linear filtered) because its extra-texture cache keys on object identity and would churn a device-sized texture per paint. Stand-alone renders are untouched (goldens byte-identical; the server catalog serves the flag). Verified in the frame lab on a rotated rounded rectangle over the stress fixture: rim, inward bend and the upper-right highlight run along the rectangle. **By eye in Julien's own frame: owed.**

### Glass lens — a round pane of thick glass over the picture — LANDED 2026-09-10 (`f58802b47`)

**What:** `glass_lens`, a lens-category catalog effect ported from the Shaders.com Glass component (circle shape), read from the same preview link as Prism once Julien had swapped the design. Center (the draggable handle) and Radius place the glass; the rim pulls the picture inward over Thickness at Refraction strength and splits the colours along the bend (Aberration); Inner zoom magnifies the interior, Edge softness fades the boundary, Blur frosts it; a directional Highlight from Light angle and an even Fresnel rim each carry a colour and a softness; Outside the glass keeps the picture or goes transparent. Because it samples its input it is also a **Frame glass lens** candidate with no extra wiring: the Frame feeds the whole backdrop at frame coordinates and clips to the layer's shape afterwards, so in the Frame the effect's own Center and Radius say where the glass sits and the layer shape trims it. Light angle runs clockwise from the right as on screen (the catalog's y-up texture space needed a sign flip). Tint (three dials at zero in the design) was not ported. Proven by a server render, targeted goldens, and four browser-harness renders through the app's real uniform path: the design's dark backdrop reads as the reference (thin rim, highlight arc at the upper right), and a striped backdrop shows the refraction, colour split, frosting, zoom and cutout. **Not yet judged by eye in a Frame.**

### Prism — a white beam that splits into a rainbow fan — LANDED 2026-09-10 (`0cecd9904`)

**What:** `prism`, a Shader Studio effect in the generative category, ported from the Shaders.com Prism component (its "Fluid Chrome" preset is the default). A thin white beam runs from a Source point (the draggable centre handle) to a Split point, then fans into a rainbow that widens with Spread and fades with End falloff; Beam width, Intensity, Beam colour, Start falloff, Softness and Saturation are the other dials. Hue drifts on the clock, so the fill wrapper's Speed scales it and there is no per-effect speed dial. The beam adds over whatever it is given, so it works as a fill on any surface and as light over an image; with no input it emits straight alpha. Source and Split Y use the catalog's y-up convention so the handle lands where you drag. Proven by a server render, targeted goldens at 128 and 256, and four renders through the app's real uniform path in the browser harness (defaults, moved points, white-only wide fan, tinted beam at low intensity). **Not yet judged by eye against the reference** — the fan reads more saturated than Shaders.com's; tune the constants before adding dials.

**How a Shaders.com design was read:** the editor URL is login-only, but a `/preview/<uuid>` share link exposes a public JSON endpoint whose `definition` is base64 of JSON XOR'd with a fixed key shipped in their bundle; the component's prop metadata and its paint graph are readable in the same bundle.

### Holographic fill — a generative sticker-vinyl shader + a one-click fill preset — LANDED 2026-09-06 (`d18c5bd72`..`489567a3a`, 20 commits, subagent-driven)

**What:** `holographic_surface`, a Shader Studio effect in the generative category that needs no input image: the pale iridescent sheet stock of holographic stickers and laminates, matched by eye against three reference photos over ~15 probe rounds. Four surfaces (Soft sweep, Watercolour bloom, Sweep and sparkle, Defined bands), dials for Scale, Iridescence, Bands, View angle, Shimmer, Silver wash, Sheen, **Glow** (a "paint" ↔ "light" mix that keeps hues bright *and* saturated without clipping to white), **Crinkle** (cellular facets with thin crease lines; the dial's full stop is an internal 0.4 — above that it reads as wet stone) and Foil tint. Three look decisions are constants, not dials: two offset rainbows screen-blended over the first (OVERLAP 0.7) so colours overlap translucently; the sweep bends along long wavy bands (BAND_SHAPE 0.8); the silver always carries a pale version of the local hue (the reference has no neutral grey anywhere). In the Frame's fill control and Space Type's slot list, **Holographic** is a 12th picker entry that writes a `type: 'shader'` fill from `HOLOGRAPHIC_FILL_PRESET` — deliberately not a `FILL_TYPES` member (twelve modules read that constant and a leak from it once drew QR codes on a 3D texture). Vector Type correctly omits it: a generative field exports as raster. The older `holographic` overlay effect keeps its id and is relabelled "Holographic Overlay".

**Guards:** a variance test per surface (spatial std per channel — the first version measured whole-array std and a flat tinted frame passed it), an input-independence test, a "never samples the input outside the mix gate" test, a seed-range test (the seed is folded `mod(seed, 97) + floor(seed/97)·0.3711`: small enough that `hash21` never quantises, distinct across the app's 0–9999 range, seed 42 unchanged), goldens at 128/256, and a preset spec that pins `FILL_TYPES` at 11 and the picker's type detection (`fillPickerType`).

**What the reviews caught:** three descending `smoothstep(hi, lo, x)` calls (undefined in GLSL ES 3.00; rewritten as `1 − smoothstep(lo, hi, x)`, bit-identical), a shimmer clock `floor(u_time·6)·1.7` that quantised to nothing after ~1 h of wall time (Shape Studio drives shader fills with wall-clock seconds; now folded like the seed), the 97-field seed collapse, and a picker that could not leave Holographic for plain Shader (the `!fill.shader` guard kept the holographic spec; now reseeded). All fixed with goldens unchanged; live-verified in WebGL2 on all four surfaces.

**Found on the way, not ours:** the golden test called `resolve_params` where its generator calls `to_uniforms(resolve_params(…))`, so any colour-param effect would have crashed it — fixed; and the suite is red at HEAD on `crystal_prism`, `oil_paint`, `blinds` (the loop stops at the first, so pytest never reaches the later effects; a non-stopping sweep of all 150 comparisons confirms those three and nothing else). Also: the :3000 dev server on this machine serves the Phase-1 worktree, not the main checkout — live checks go to :3002.

**Process note:** the shader plan supplied reference GLSL to validate, not code to transcribe (the previous plan on this repo shipped a transcribed routine that changed zero pixels). The look was settled by contact-sheet probes against the user's photos; twice the build veered into unasked features (brush streaks, body shading) and was pulled back to the last accepted tile — the anchor rule.

### playgrnd's five new tools — Carve, Culture, Chaff, Strand, Husk — LANDED 2026-09-05 (`8c7754c0f`..`6032d0720`, 14 commits, subagent-driven)

Julien: "Playground got some new tools, can you take a look." Read from source before placing, and placed by the Frame UX rule (elements from the Shapes menu; the inspector tunes the selection; nothing creation-shaped in the panels): **Carve** is a panel composition, so it is a seventh **Mosaic style** (recursive splits, treatments per panel — flat / two-pitch stripes with the dropped-signal break / stacked chevrons / a photographic grain ramp / one hairline grid — six-ink palettes as presets). **Culture** is a pure field, so it is a **Shader Studio generative** (`culture`: summed bumps that fuse into colonies, a radius-placed ring ladder of ink roles, band + colour grain, drift / grow / cycle motion, five plates as Looks) and therefore a Frame fill for free. **Chaff, Strand and Husk** are scatters of thrown marks, not compositions, so they became a new **Scatter element** — a sibling of Mosaic in the Shapes menu with a Style control — built as a registry (`lib/compositor/scatter.ts`: a style row carries defaults, normalize, palette presets, a control list the inspector renders itself, and a paint function), so the second and third styles each cost one module plus one line at each anchor and no inspector code. Chaff = arcs with a width profile (crescent / leaf / bar) printed through a two-scale mottled mask with an `apart` knockout; Strand = branching random-walk rod chains with a notch at every joint, each rod its own hand-drawn outline, a misregistered plate behind, stipple / drag / screen dropout pooling in the core; Husk = sine-warped ovals as a max-combined depth field, the fill eaten by a crumble or a dot screen. The agent got a `scatter` op with readback and Carve on the `mosaic` op's one precedence rule.

**What the reviews caught this time** (every port was read against its source as code and found faithful — Strand's 7000-rod corner was timed IN the tool at 3232 ms too): comment prose copied or structurally paraphrased from the unlicensed reference, four tasks in a row — verbatim shingle checks pass while clause skeletons and distinctive images survive synonym swaps, so a reviewer now reads comment-by-comment, and the dispatch names the banned images; one un-sourced `min(1, slim·pad)` clamp that halved Chaff's Separation margin; a stated fact about the tool that was wrong (Husk's export is a 600–6000 slider, not a fixed 2400); and host integration — Carve's grain was recomputed per pixel every redraw with a device-pixel tooth, so preview and bake differed, fixed with a memoized panel and a box-space lattice (tooth = 1/2400 of the box width, floored at one device pixel), now the rule for every per-pixel sheet; Husk's field aspect now comes from the box, not the rounded buffer. A generic Shader Studio fix rode along: the preview never ran a clock for a still source, so any effect's Speed / Motion dial was inert in the studio — `lib/shaderstudio/clock.ts` starts one when a *visible* time dial is above zero. The whole-branch review (live UX check on `/dev/frame-lab`, 636/636 branch specs, typecheck at baseline, all 57 unrelated suite failures attributed to other sessions) asked for one fix before "done" — Carve's grain cache was the only one without a byte budget — and left two follow-ups as chips: share the four-times-copied per-pixel sheet shell (`ctxScale` / box-space cell / 6 Mpx cap / memo shell) so a sixth style costs one generator, and make the guards durable (the prose shingle script into `scripts/`, a compositor hint budget test with the mosaic hint rebuilt from data, a pin on the Look-matching array branch). **Pane ink controls (`73925df87`, same day, on Julien's note "pane needs color controls"):** the Pane style only had a Palette preset select where the tool shows its ordered palette as swatches; the inspector now shows the eight inks as swatches in palette order (Spread is a distance along that order), one edit keeps the other seven, a vocab-driven Pane pins the vocabulary's colours on its first edit, and the picker's alpha is dropped — `paneInkPatch` is pure and tested; live-checked (first ink → green repainted the panes). **Totem (`45535880d` + fix `bff9bb52d`, same day, on Julien's "how would you integrate this one?" → "go for it"):** read whole, it is a composition, not the vector piece the 09-04 mapping guessed — a matted, keylined plate whose left half is carved into regions (cut the largest, ≥ 6 units a side), each dealt one of eleven two-colour cell motifs and mirrored to the right (Mirror = the chance the twin keeps the deal), with a stack of nested rings at the centre; the mat is a majority-smoothed speckle in the border band; ink roles by luminance and swatch order. It is the eighth Mosaic style with ten dials, six presets and the five inks as ordered swatches. Reviewed against the source rule by rule with the RNG draw order preserved: no look-changing deviation; one declared host cap on the unit count (a 6000×300 box at the finest grain) proven inert across the tool's whole export range; the review's findings were prose (a clause-for-clause paraphrase, the reference's 'nested emblem at dead centre' under one-word swaps) and an alpha inconsistency, all fixed. Follow-up: a true SVG export by replaying the paint into a rectangle recorder, as the tool does. **Blueprint (`035bf1c71` + look fix `966f8f2a8`, on Julien's "can we use the mosaic feature to generate technical grids" → "fit it into the mosaic shape"):** a NINTH Mosaic style, and the first that isn't a playgrnd port — a technical drafting grid built from a reference image. A dark ground under a minor/major cartesian grid, plus a polar overlay struck from a seeded origin: dashed radial spokes at an angle step, concentric arcs with tick marks, and angle labels (the first Mosaic style to draw arcs and text). Ten-ish dials, five palette presets, New variation rolls the origin corner and offsets while the grid stays deterministic. Reviewed Approved: contract-clean (box space, no absolute globalAlpha/op, dash always reset, text inside the clip, seeded), Carve/Totem wiring complete, behavioural tests. The build's default came out mint-green with an invisible minor grid; fixed to yellow-on-green with both grids reading out of the box, matching the reference. Confirms the Mosaic "shape" absorbs a line-and-text composition as cheaply as a rectangle one — a new element would have cost the whole local-kind scaffolding on top. **Center origin (`ee7ff2745`, on Julien's "add concentric circles, draw from the center"):** the polar overlay could only hang off a corner (a quarter protractor); a `center` option on the origin dial strikes it from the box centre, the fan base becomes 0 (angleStart the only rotation), and with Spread 360 the arcs close into full concentric circles with radial spokes all around — a polar / radar plot. Deterministic at the centre (no seeded offset), as a diagram should be. **Per-type stroke width + line style (`fe020a7c2`, on Julien's "for each type can i set the stroke width and type?"):** width and dash were baked per element; now spokes, arcs and ticks each have a width multiplier, the grid, spokes, arcs and ticks each have a solid/dashed select, and a dash-scale dial tunes the pattern. Defaults preserve the original look exactly; every dashed stroke resets after so nothing leaks past the overlay. Known and accepted: bakes above 6 Mpx upsample the sheet; agent history labels and `setSize` height skip both Mosaic and Scatter alike; Husk's dither / finish stage on the tool page is outside the captured generator.

### playgrnd ports, done right: six line-faithful generators + the Mosaic element — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`7c575d0da`..`6ebcf2367`)

A correction first. The grid templates and the first Pane shipped earlier today were built from written descriptions of the playgrnd tools, not their code, and the user called it: "these don't look at all like the playground tools… you've made wide decisions without checking the code, and it's openly available." Reading all six generators completely (their full `paint()` functions, captured as reference files) showed the "grid family shares one coarse grid" premise was wrong: they are six distinct generators — Pane is row-masonry of two-ink HSL-walked gradient cells; Modular is a flush module grid of weighted tile TYPES (empty heaviest); Oddgrid a 44-col fbm pixel field with blockiness, density and motifs; Parcel a two-tone fbm block field with multiply hairline survey grids (not rounded rects); Static an 84-res one-bit poster of stacked pattern bands plus a glitch pass; Mosh six bands of column-quantized rectangles from a shuffled deck of failure kinds. The discipline that finally works: capture the whole generator, reimplement from it with a rule-by-rule checklist mapped to lines (own hash/fbm/GLSL — the site has no license), render character images and judge them against the tool, then have a reviewer read the port against the source *as code*, because parity only proves two renderers agree.

**Pane** (`7c575d0da`, deal): rebuilt as a faithful port — row-masonry with a per-row varying cell count, corner/edge direction vectors with `diag` biasing flats vs diagonals, inks `spread` steps apart in palette order, `soft` ramp extent, an HSL short-arc ramp with 8 interior stops and the achromatic hue-borrow. Six source dials in the inspector, a one-click Pane at the source defaults, `pane` on the `dealGrid` op. The review confirmed all 11 rules; its two hollow tests were hardened (`b01ed0898`). **Oddgrid** (`4fdb66e95`, Shader Studio): a generative frag reproducing the coverage/colour/fine fields, block-centre sliding, value→palette-index, specks and ink motifs in snapped cell space, with exact browser/server parity and the tool's five LOOKS via a new per-effect Look select. Homes were decided from the code: rectangle/1-bit generators (Modular, Parcel, Mosh) go on the deal's seeded canvas painter; per-pixel fields (Oddgrid, and Static — whose glitch ops are a fixed seeded set, resolvable per fragment) go to Shader Studio and gain fills. **Programme complete the same day.** Modular (`ba4c962574`), Parcel (`5ce0ceea3`) and Mosh (`28bf8eb54`) landed on the deal, Static (`1308b98c6`) in Shader Studio — every one reviewed against its captured source rule by rule with zero look-changing deviations in any algorithm (the Static reviewer simulated the sequential glitch buffer against the per-fragment walk: 0 mismatches over 377,876 cells; Mosh coverage proven over 480 layouts). What the reviews caught instead were host-integration bugs, now fixed and worth remembering for any canvas-painting mode: absolute ctx-state writes stomp the layer's own opacity/blend (scale and restore to the incoming value); the tools rely on the canvas edge to clip oversized rects, so a deal box needs its own clip; fixed op-count caps must cover the largest real grid; raw layer objects reach the paint path un-normalised. Cleanup: the misnamed coarse-grid templates were retired on both surfaces (`e945fdc61` — the Frame keeps four faithful one-click generators, Pattern Studio's dealt-grid presets are now honestly structural: Even/Loose/Dense/Packed/Fine), every deal mode is clipped to its box (`1dcec34e7`), and Pane got its ordered palette with the tool's five lists (`5cc5e8bff`) — a fresh Pane now reads in the tool's pink/blue/orange/lavender/white register, not the brand rainbow. A closing cross-cutting review of the shared deal surfaces (which five implementers had edited in sequence) found the paint branch and state contract coherent and fixed the seams between them (`332ed467a`): one mode-precedence rule for the agent's create and reconfigure paths, a deal that the agent can actually read back (fill, dials, seed, preset), agent-created deals that fill the frame instead of a square (every generator derives its rows from the box aspect), presets scoped to the chosen fill with a helpful error instead of silently recolouring another mode, and the vocabulary palette control shown only when it drives the look.

**The Mosaic element (`e0b9119ba`, `13e9c9142`, `3feff5053`; review fixes `6ebcf2367`).** The user's UX correction closed the programme properly: "it would make more sense to ship these compositions as a Mosaic element in the shapes menu, rather than a generator on the right panel." The rule now: elements are added from the toolbar Shapes menu, the inspector tunes the selection, the no-selection panel tunes the frame (its Grid section is the layout guide again), and creation never lives in the inspector. A composition is ONE Mosaic element with a **Style** control — Tiles / Pane / Modular / Parcel / Mosh / Oddgrid / Static — each style's dials and Palette presets beneath it, New variation re-rolling one seed for all of them; the two shader styles paint through the shader-as-fill path (a locked `ShaderFillEditor` with the effect's Looks as its Palette), the agent has a `mosaic` op with one precedence rule and a readback. The review read every render path (inline, effects, masked, motion, bake, thumbnail, selection PNG) and found the shader-as-fill bridge clean but the semantics wrong: a frame-anchored field was a window the box slid over, and any paint outside a stack span read a stale frame base — Oddgrid/Static thumbnails were a flat swatch. Fixed by making the box the shader's frame (the deal branch swaps the field context's frame size and base for the box while it paints; the pre-pass requests the field at box size), so the field renders at the box's own aspect, sits on the box, travels with it and repeats per cloner copy; per-effect specs are stashed so a hop away and back keeps the dials; the Image-mix dial is hidden with the Input it mixes. **Next, approved and in progress:** the five new playgrnd tools read from source — Carve as a Mosaic style, Chaff/Strand/Husk as a sibling *Scatter* element, Culture as a Shader Studio generative (plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-04-playgrnd-five-new-tools.md`).

### Grid templates (slice 3a) — the playgrnd grid family as one-click presets, both surfaces — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`74f67bbb4`, `024f5c37c`, `238c5b328`, `5977a0e73`)

The five playgrnd grid/composition tools (Modular, Oddgrid, Parcel, Mosh, Static — which share one generator in playgrnd) become one-click presets on BOTH deal engines, from one shared registry `lib/frame/gridTemplates.ts`. Each template carries two engine configs (the Frame generative deal + Pattern Studio's tileable dealt-grid) because the two seed differently; values read from each tool's actual composition, not slider names. Frame deal: a picker in the deal inspector + a create-from-template path + a `template` arg on the `dealGrid` agent op (template pre-fills, explicit args override). Pattern Studio: a Template picker + a new colour-vocabulary axis (`dgVocab` brand/mono/warm/cool) so presets recolour — four role families over ONE shared `DEALTGRID_VOCABS` table read by both the GLSL uniform path and the TS sampler, so the twin stays in parity by construction (measured Δ 0.0 across vocabs, ≈0.6 templates, unchanged). A caught gap worth noting: a select control that only stores its value is a dead control — the dealt-grid Template picker rendered but did nothing until `setParam` (in both the studio surface and the texture agent surface) was intercepted to expand it into the live dials, with an exact multi-key undo. User chose curated-presets-now; user-saveable templates are a deferred fast-follow.

### playgrnd batch — grid slice 2 (generative deal) + Pattern Studio dealt-grid + hue-walk ramp — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`4632b6d40`+`7aeebe139`, `ab9b7485e`+`84420e53f`, `434b9e207`)

Four-item batch continuing the playgrnd-tools port (item 1 = the grid slice-1 browser verification, confirmed live). **Hue-walk ramp** (`lib/color/hue.ts`+`hueWalk.ts`): 2-colour gradients can interpolate hue along the SHORT or LONG OKLCH arc (orange→blue the long way goes through green, not grey) via a stop-expander that bakes N walked stops, so canvas + shader + LUT backends all show the walk unchanged; wired as an Interpolation control (Direct | Hue short | Hue long) in `compositor/GradientEditor.vue`. Review caught + fixed a Critical: `gradientFromPaint` (the sole gradient read-back normalizer) silently stripped the interp fields → the control reverted to Direct and eventually baked over the author stops. **Generative deal (grid slice 2)**: ONE self-painting `deal` Compositor layer fills a modular grid's cells from a seeded weighted vocabulary (`lib/compositor/dealVocab.ts`, brand/mono/warm/cool) — a dense decorative grid as one baking layer instead of N; `grid.gen.seed` drives layout + independent keep/fill streams so density never reshuffles fills and "New variation" re-rolls coherently. Box math is WIDTH-normalized (boxW,boxH both `*W`); force-keep guarantees a sparse deal never renders blank. Agent `dealGrid` op + Deal-grid button/inspector. **Pattern Studio dealt-grid**: a 6th Texture FX `MODES` entry — a seeded, tileable rigid grid-of-cells modeled on chips (role-by-hash, density drop + force-keep, size variance), implemented as the required TS sampler + GLSL twin with measured parity Δ=0.6. Each item got a per-task adversarial review; disjoint file sets (color / compositor / texturefx); 71 combined unit tests.

### Compositor — brush layers resize with the selection handles — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`577bd09bb`, `4477d6648`)

User wanted to drag a brush layer's handles to make the painted shape bigger or smaller; chose uniform "keep proportions" (per-axis stretch would distort the round dabs). A brush's stored `w` (width fraction) now drives a uniform scale `= layer.w / naturalW` (naturalW = the extent of `strokeBounds(strokes)`); height is derived from the strokes' natural aspect, so brush joins `wired` in `aspectLockedResizeKind` (corner-only, aspect-locked). `drawBrush` and `localLayerBox` both apply the same scale, so the box, handles, hit-test and rendered strokes (thickness included) scale together; at paint-commit `w === naturalW` so an un-resized brush is byte-identical. Both the modal and the Frame card get the handles (shared gate). The review caught one real bug before merge: a frame-anchored shader fill's `_fieldCtx.base` DOMMatrix carried the scale on its translate but not its `scaleSelf` (fill drifted off a resized brush) — fixed to `scaleSelf(dpr*scale)`; only reachable at scale≠1, so byte-identical tests couldn't catch it. Live-verified on a throwaway: corner handles appear, box scales exactly 2×/0.5× with `w`, proportions kept. Known limits (out of scope): the brush MASK isn't scaled (same systemic limit as other kinds), and painting more strokes onto a resized brush re-fits it.

### Compositor — panning the modal is smooth (live-preview loop paused during pan/zoom) — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`9eb0bdd6b`, `e5dd438f5`; reverts `111c3a4d1`)

User: panning the Compositor stage is "mega slow." A pan mutates only `view.tx/ty` (a CSS transform) and nothing watches `view`, so it triggers no re-render — but the modal runs an always-on live-preview loop (`liveFrameTick`) that composites the whole stack (wired studio pull + every shader fill) at full device resolution every animation frame (~19 fps even idle on a shader-heavy frame). Panning needs the frame budget to composite the transform and the loop steals it, collapsing to ~5 fps. Fix: a `viewMoving` flag (set on pan pointer-down + move, wheel/trackpad pan, and pinch/⌘-zoom; self-clears 180 ms after the last change) makes the loop skip its pull+composite for the duration of the gesture and resume the instant it ends — the same loop-gating the Frame card already had, which the modal lacked. **Perf follow-up (`9f0f96a06`, `b4a809e58`):** ported the card's live-preview resolution cap (LIVE_PREVIEW_MAXPX 640k px; ~3.2M→0.8M px during animation), content-fps throttle (repaint on frame-index change, 30fps for shader-only, not display refresh), and resize-only-when-changed guard — idle went 19→27 fps on the user's shader-heavy frame; the remaining cost is a per-frame torn-edge readback on the shader-fill brush (torn edge + animated fill = uncached, re-torn every frame), left parked. Marking on pointer-down (not just first move) removes the one-in-flight-render delay at gesture start. User-confirmed "a lot smoother." **Two wrong turns first, recorded so they aren't repeated:** suppressing the glass-panel `backdrop-filter` blur did nothing, and promoting the stage with `will-change: transform` **crashed the tab** — a large GPU layer over this frame's live WebGL shader is a VRAM cost that turned lag into an "Aw snap"; reverted. **Could not measure this locally** — the in-app browser pane runs hidden, which pauses the very rAF loop at fault (and throttles timers), and no Chrome extension was connected; the always-on loop was found only via a copy-paste DevTools probe the user ran in their visible tab. **Still open:** the modal still animates at ~19 fps idle because the live preview composites at full resolution; the Frame card's live-resolution cap is not yet ported to the modal.

### Canvas — node drags no longer re-walk the whole graph every frame — LANDED 2026-09-04 (`bc88f0a41`)

Julien: "dragging these nodes around the canvas lags A LOT." Measured on a copy of his project (6-8 nodes, 3-5 edges): **~250-300 ms per pointer move**, all inside Vue's reactive flush — no canvas, WebGL, layout or JSON work; the DOM only moved the dragged node and redrew the edge paths. A per-effect profile of `VueNodeCanvas.vue` put it in the deep-watch getters: `watch([nodes, edges], …, { deep: true })` re-traverses (and re-tracks) every reactive property of every node's `data` on every position change (~30 ms per frame with no edges), and because Vue Flow's edge objects hold `sourceNode`/`targetNode`, each of the two `watch(edges, …, { deep: true })` was another full-graph walk — three per frame. Fix: the graph deep-watch (undo snapshot + autosave dirty) is **paused for the duration of a node drag** (Vue 3.5 watch handle; `onNodeDragStart` → pause, `onNodeDragStop` → resume, which re-evaluates once and yields the drag's single history step and dirty signal; window `pointerup`/`pointercancel`/`blur` resume as a safety net), and the two edge watchers now key on a pure **edge topology string** (`lib/canvas/edgeTopologyKey.ts`: id, source, source handle, target, target handle) instead of the whole graph — they only ever cared about wiring. Live after: **0.4-4 ms per move**, no long tasks, one dirty at drag start + one at drop. Unit-tested (13). Remaining per-frame cost: the groups/positions watch is O(nodes) string work — fine at this scale. Probe method worth keeping: wrap every component's `effect.fn` / `scope.effects[*].fn` from `app._instance` to time watchers and computeds per move; `app.config.performance` does nothing under Nuxt dev.

### Compositor — torn edge / feather no longer re-tear on every drag — LANDED 2026-09-03 (`fd3c1cc1d`, `7c456d704`, `14d54468f`, `06f9bb850`, `8c4ccd9e5`)

Julien reported that moving one Brush layer in a Frame "lags a ton" and wondered if it was his machine. Measured on his real project: **~230 ms per pointer move**, ~160 ms of it `applyTornEdge` (the per-pixel distance-transform + fbm pass over the whole device-res layer bitmap) re-run on every repaint although only x/y changed — and run **twice**, because the Frame card hidden behind the modal repaints on the same layer watch (~110 ms on its 1084² canvas) as the modal (~45 ms). The Shapes fill tile was already cached and innocent. Two fixes: (1) `paintLayer` now bakes an eligible layer's local box once — content → torn edge → feather — into a small raster cached by content signature + raster size (`lib/compositor/silhouetteCache.ts`, LRU 24, 16 Mpx per-raster cap) and later repaints just draw it under the layer's transform; ineligible cases (wired, corner-pin, DOF, inner shadow, chain effects, shader fills, unready fonts/images, expressive text, clones with a step scale) keep the byte-identical old path. A 2 px transparent margin around the raster is load-bearing: the tear/feather measure distance to the nearest transparent pixel, and a tight box would silently no-op both. (2) `createOcclusionRepaintGate()` in `lib/studio/occlusion.ts` makes the Frame card swallow watch-driven repaints while any fullscreen modal covers the canvas and paint exactly once on close. Live-verified in the app: the same layer drags at **~27 ms/move with zero torn passes**; hidden-card repaints during a 6-move drag 6 → 0, 1 on close; rotated feathered rect, torn text (A/B against the uncached path), scaled clones and an over-cap raster all render. One visible change: the tear/feather noise is now sampled in the layer's own box, so a tear travels with its layer instead of re-sampling at each new frame position. Unit-tested (silhouette cache 27, repaint gate 5). The final whole-branch review caught the raster pad ignoring centre-aligned strokes (the default), line round caps and text height boxes — all three clipped ink in preview and export; fixed with one pure ink-overhang helper, plus a per-side canvas limit, a 192 MB byte budget on the cache and an exact 1:1 stamp, and A/B-verified live against the uncached path; the re-review then closed the last gap of the same class (a single word wider than its fixed text box). Pre-existing and NOT fixed: the corner-pin offscreen has the same clipping for centre strokes and `boxH` text. Deferred: per-paint `JSON.stringify` of point-heavy brush layers on the key path. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-torn-edge-silhouette-cache.md`.

### playgrnd backgrounds → Shader Studio — plan 1 of 6 LANDED 2026-09-03 (`b16e07804`..`8601708f7`, 18 commits)

Julien adores [playgrnd.tools](https://www.playgrnd.tools/) — 33 tiny seeded canvas generators in five kinds (Backgrounds 11, Patterns 9, Posters 9, Textures 3, Badges 1), each with a handful of dials, an **ink list read as a ramp**, frame ratios, a Variation button, a dither pass, a captures strip and PNG/SVG/video export. The site carries no license, so this is a port of ideas, never code. The mapping agreed the same day: Backgrounds and Textures → Shader Studio generative frags (this plan); Pane and Rise → Gradient Studio presets; the tiling Patterns → Pattern Studio families; Emblem, Tokens, Oddgrid → Shape Studio arrangements; Riso → a Frame preset over the torn-edge effect; the nine Posters plus Sampler → a **poster surface that does not exist** (new Poster Studio or the Elements gallery — Julien's call). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-playgrnd-backgrounds-shader-catalog.md`.

**Why Shader Studio first:** shader is already a fill type in the Frame (fill, stroke and background) and in every studio through the field module, so a catalog entry lands in every fill picker with no further work. **What landed:** six new generative frags — `terrain_bands` (banded contour landscape), `sonar` (dithered landmasses, two inks and a stippled shore), `mist` (sprayed neon washes screen-blended over the paper ink), `pixel_bloom` (mirrored, quantised pixel field; Totem is the same effect at 9:16), `filament` (flowing thread field, one ink per thread), `sear` (banded thermal field with row drag, tear and streak) — each driven by a `gradient` manifest param used as playgrnd's ink ramp; three shaders that had been on disk since 08-11 with no manifest entry registered (`topographic`, `stipple`, `pixel_sort`); look-words for all nine plus three chain looks (pixel aurora, ascii terrain, corrupted satellite) with the guidance at 7601/8000; and a Playwright proof that `terrain_bands` as a Frame layer fill paints the field, not the fallback gradient (diff against the same layer filled with that gradient, 0→1→2→still-2 card identity, max-channel diff, positive band-count assertion). Every effect has server goldens at 128/256, browser/server parity at 0/255, and the uniform contract test green.

**What the reviews caught in the plan's own GLSL** (subagent-driven, a review per task, a final whole-branch review): the pixel_bloom mirror was only exact on square frames (cell count fractional on the long axis → now rounded to whole cells, floored at 2); filament's ink index used `floor(f)` and split every thread's colour down its centre (→ round to nearest); sonar's `smoothstep` had equal edges at zero shore width (→ guarded); sear's row-break clock ignored Speed (→ scaled, golden-neutral by arithmetic). Mist at its first defaults read as a mesh gradient, not spray — ink lookup now starts at the second stop, spray hashed once per pixel (71 → 50 ms at 1080p), defaults retuned. Harness timing at 1080p incl. readback: sear 17 ms, terrain 18, sonar 14, bloom 12, filament 33, mist 50, against 12 for the existing fbm.

**Frame grid & sections — Swiss layout substrate, slice 1 of 4 (`2ee1ee67f`..`c49532438`):** the Frame gains a modular grid — explicit (columns/rows/gutter/margin) or generated within constraints with a regularity dial (0 free … 1 equal) and a seed with New-variation — that layers snap to. A "section" is not a new type: it is any layer whose box aligns to the grid, filled with anything (shader/gradient/shape/image/text), overlapping by z-order. Built subagent-driven, a review per task + an opus whole-branch review: a pure grid engine (`lib/frame/grid.ts`, seeded dealer + merge + mirror), the snap function extended with grid-line targets, a `sailor_localGrid` frame property (deep-defaulted so old frames are off/unchanged), an editor-only overlay that never bakes, the inspector, drag-snapping, marquee draw-from-grid, a capped (≤24) "fill grid with sections", per-section re-snap, and an agent `setGrid` op. 32 unit tests. The per-task reviews caught **two real Criticals in the regularity dial** (r=1 didn't equalise for odd counts; convergence wasn't monotonic — both from per-edge module snapping; fixed by a width-space lerp to equal positions) that the first tests missed, and the final review caught three more (agent op declared-not-wired, dead gutter slider, unclamped margin) — all fixed. OWED: the whole-slice browser visual pass, blocked by a pre-existing unrelated `VT_STRETCH_MAX` main crash. Slices 2–4 (generative deal, templates, editorial + Smart Layout) consume this substrate. Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-frame-grid-sections-design.md`.

**Shader seed / Variation (`7a871c65b`..`5fa4c1c4d`):** every generative shader used to render a hardcoded `u_seed: 42`, so the field, tears, specks and grain were identical on every document — playgrnd gives each tool one seed with a New button and re-roll. Added a `seed` to the Shader Studio config and to the shader-fill `ShaderSpec`, threaded to `u_seed` on both the studio preview (`passes.ts`) and the fill path (`field.ts`), with a New-variation button and an editable number ("Variation") in both panels and an agent control on both surfaces. The load-bearing part: `seed` is folded into BOTH `fieldKey` and `specIdentityKey` in `shaderfill/descriptor.ts`, so two variations never collide in the field cache (the silent-wrong-pixels class that file guards against) — pinned by a new unit test. Existing documents are unchanged: a saved config or spec without a seed hydrates to 42 via deepMerge / normalizeShaderSpec. Harness-verified: the frags differ 35–61% between seed 42 and seed 7, and seed 42 reproduces identically. Review caught a build break — two `ShaderSpec` literals in a dev-bench page that bypass the normaliser needed the now-required field — fixed.

**Correction, same evening (`d5513f21c`..`a0c663af4`, 12 commits):** Julien: "looks like you kinda guessed what each slider does and didn't look at the actual code". True — plan 1 was written from slider names and hint text. Each tool's page carries its paint code as plain JavaScript, so every generator was re-read from source and rebuilt (own GLSL, nothing copied). What changed: **Sear** is now two passes — a heat field with the tool's two-stage warp and rough fbm (gain 0.58, lacunarity 2.05), histogram flattening with the frame mean and spread measured from a fixed 8×8 grid, then per row the tool's Drag (smooth pull-back), Tear (2.8%-high bands that collapse and wrap-shift) and Streak (a run-length hold, emulated per pixel by a 16-tap backward scan on an absolute lattice with the tool's anchor-deviation rule; the reviewer's ground-truth harness puts it at 77% pixel-identical to the tool's sequential scan at the default, up from 46%). **Sonar** is the tool's cell grid: four ink roles, one land square per cell growing with depth, two-colour specks along the cut. **Pixel Bloom** is rings from the middle on a mirrored cell grid, pushed by two fields, with a quiet centre and a wrapping ramp — not a noise field. **Mist** is a pastel blot ground (base inks weighted by fbm^3.4, blended in squared colour) under a curtain of vertical neon streaks with soft cover. **Terrain Bands** has one band per ink, straight grain, Spread and Blockiness. **Filament** was a misnomer — the real tool traces thousands of strands through a flow field and draws them as polylines with marks, which no fragment shader can do — so the iso-line look shipped under that name is renamed **Thread Contours**, with a legacy id alias for saved layers and graphs (frontend lookups and the ComfyUI combo both accept `filament`). Method going forward: read the paint function before writing a port.

**Sonar v2 (same day, `ace3dfb07`..`61f2b3a28`):** Julien showed playgrnd's Sonar carrying six inks and asked for more colour. Rebuilt: the `gradient` param is read as **ink roles** (ground, land, coast fringe, specks, deep land, shallows) rather than a ramp; the land is an ordered-dither halftone in snapped-pixel space that is solid inland and thins to scattered squares at the coast; a dithered fringe and a wider shallows band sit off the shore; specks scatter on both sides. Control groups mirror the reference: Land (Coverage, Feature size, Distortion), Halftone (cells, depth), Coast (fringe, specks). Reviewed as code: the first cut drew lattice lines (halftone grid beating the pixel grid) and the reviewer then caught an ink fallback that painted the sea in the land colour at two inks, a Coverage slider dead at both ends, and a deep-ink seam at high depth — all fixed; Coverage now maps onto the field's measured 0.3..0.7 band.

**Owed:** one small paid call — "pixel aurora background" should land aurora and name bayer_dither as the by-hand second layer; a look at the nine new panel entries in the Shader Studio UI itself (render correctness is proven by goldens, panel layout is not). **Pre-existing, not from this work:** `crystal_prism` golden fails at HEAD (54/255, GPU drift) and the `distort` goldens are untracked, so the golden suite is red on a clean checkout. **Headroom:** 399 chars under the guidance ceiling — Plan 3 (Pattern Studio families) should budget a trim.

### Die Doing techniques — Shape Blend phase one + 3D Screen finish LANDED 2026-09-03; shared motion moves DESIGNED

Julien adores Carsten Gueth (@die_doing): hundreds of thin outlines stepping from one shape to another so a gradient appears from line density and breaks into moiré up close; spheres and blobs whose shading is made of dots that wrap the form, shrink into shadow, fringe red and blue, and dissolve into the ground. An inventory of Sailor at HEAD showed the dot-screened forms are mostly reachable today (3D Studio → gradient material → post-stack Dot screen + Chroma, or wired into Shader Studio's risograph), while two things were missing. Both are now specified, plus a third that fell out of the second.

**Shape Blend — phase one LANDED** (`superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-shape-blend-design.md` §1–10; plan `superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-shape-blend-phase-one.md`, 10 tasks, subagent-driven with a review per task and a final whole-branch review). Shape Studio has a fourth layout, **Blend**: the steps from shape A to a new shape B (kind, size, rotation, offset), with **Spacing** (even / ease in / ease out / ease in-out) and **Twist** (rotates which point of A meets which point of B). Two colour options landed for every layout: **Colour ramp** (the fills list read as a smooth perceptual fade across the copies, OKLab via `mixHex`) and **Colour applies to** fill / outline / both; the stroke slider steps by 0.25 so hairlines are reachable. The engine is a pure module, `lib/vector/morph.ts` (streaming path lexer for `M L H V C S Q T A Z`, arcs → cubics, flatten, arc-length resample, winding fix + best start offset + twist, `prepareBlend` once per render → one closure per step; exact control-point interpolation when the two shapes share a command skeleton and twist is 0; samples per outline scale down above three subpaths), plus `lib/color/ramp.ts`. `composite()` takes one `d` per clone; `styled()` routes all three emit sites so the default paint target renders byte-identically (a review caught the single-mode overlap piece gaining a stroke — fixed with regression tests). The agent recipe ("stacked outlines", "die doing", "moiré lines") lands in one turn: the shape adapter now applies the gating fields (`layout`, `fillStrategy`, `paintTarget`) before re-validating the rest. Re-roll rolls a `blend` group; paint stays curated. Live-verified in the studio: circle → leaf, 150 hairline outlines, ramp, twist 0.1, offset 90 reproduces the reference; ease-in bunches the steps; switching back to Radial hides the group; headless SVG export of the same config = 150 paths, all `fill="none"`. 22 test files / 410 tests green; vue-tsc clean for every touched file. **Owed:** one live model run of the recipe (paid); phase two (Frame-native Blend layer, spec §11); motion arrives with the shared moves panel. Review notes worth keeping: the plan's reference code carried two parser bugs (S after Q/T, packed arc flags) and one regression (overlap stroke) that per-task review caught; the `timeout` binary does not exist on this machine, so a `timeout … | grep` pipeline silently prints nothing.

**Blend freeze fixed 2026-09-03 (afternoon).** Julien reported Blend "crashing erratically".
